Progress: 16 / 17 · Stage: Synthesis · Visual grammar: **arrow field with magnitudes withheld** · India hook: **the Thar margin, direction only**

### The intuition, before any term

A classification boundary is not drawn on the ground. It is drawn on a **statistic**. So when the
statistic moves, **the boundary moves — and nothing about the scheme has changed.**

That single observation is the entire analytical content of this lesson, and it is genuinely
powerful: it means a classification can be used as a **yardstick for change**, measuring with a
familiar instrument rather than a new one. Köppen's thresholds were fixed in 1936 and have not moved
since; if the map drawn with them looks different, the difference is in the data.

**And it is also the most dangerous content in the topic**, because the step from *"boundaries can
move"* to *"this boundary has moved, this far, in this direction, by this year"* is one sentence long
and requires a dataset, a baseline, a scenario and a period — **none of which exists in this
evidence base.** C1 takes that step at line 105 and contradicts two other passages of its own file in
doing so.

**So this lesson draws direction and refuses magnitude, visibly.**

### The primary visual — the arrow field

An arrow field is chosen because it can carry **direction while demonstrably withholding magnitude**.
Every arrow is unlabelled for length on purpose, and every one carries the same tag.

```text
   THRESHOLD MOBILITY — DIRECTION REASONED, MAGNITUDE WITHHELD
   ✅ Mechanism: Khullar folio 158 (the B relation) + C1 lines 171-182
   ✗ NO DATASET, BASELINE, SCENARIO OR PERIOD EXISTS IN THIS EVIDENCE BASE

   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ ① THERMAL BOUNDARIES (A/C at 18 °C, D/E at 10 °C, C/D at −3 °C)         │
   │                                                                          │
   │    cold ◄───────────────────────────────────────────────────► warm      │
   │                      ──────►  ⚠️ magnitude not stated                    │
   │    ⚠️ DIRECTION: if the relevant monthly mean rises past a threshold,    │
   │       the class on that side is assigned. POLEWARD and UPSLOPE for       │
   │       thermal limits, BY CONSTRUCTION — the threshold is a temperature.  │
   │    ⚠️ MAGNITUDE: NOT STATED. NO RATE, NO EXTENT, NO DATE.                │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ ② THE ARID BOUNDARY (B, defined by a RELATION — ✅ folio 158)            │
   │                                                                          │
   │    humid ◄──────────────────────────────────────────────────► dry       │
   │                      ──────►  ⚠️ magnitude not stated                    │
   │    ⚠️ DIRECTION: B can be assigned by EITHER pan of the balance —        │
   │       rainfall falling, OR evaporative demand rising with temperature.   │
   │       ►► THIS IS THE ONLY KÖPPEN BOUNDARY WITH TWO INDEPENDENT CAUSES    │
   │          OF MOVEMENT, which is why it is the most sensitive and the      │
   │          most over-claimed.                                              │
   │    ⚠️ MAGNITUDE: NOT STATED.                                             │
   │    ✗ NO INDIAN GEOGRAPHY IS NAMED AS MOVING. U5 REFUSED.                 │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ ③ HIGHLAND AND POLAR LIMITS (E, warmest month below 10 °C)              │
   │                                                                          │
   │    low ◄────────────────────────────────────────────────────► high      │
   │                      ──────►  ⚠️ magnitude not stated                    │
   │    ⚠️ DIRECTION: a warming warmest-month pushes the E criterion UPSLOPE; │
   │       the class CONTRACTS from below, by construction.                   │
   │    ⚠️ MAGNITUDE: NOT STATED.                                             │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   ┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
   │ ④ THE SEASONALITY LETTERS (f / m / w / s)  ── THE OVERLOOKED ONE        │
   │                                                                          │
   │    ⚠️ DIRECTION: these letters depend on WHEN rain falls, not how much.  │
   │       A regime can keep its total and change its slot-2 letter. A code   │
   │       can therefore change with NO change in annual rainfall AND no      │
   │       change in mean temperature.                                        │
   │    ⚠️ MAGNITUDE: NOT STATED. And no study of Indian seasonality shift    │
   │       was retrieved.                                                     │
   └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

   ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
   THE TAG ON EVERY ARROW IS THE CONTENT. An arrow field without magnitudes is
   not an incomplete diagram; it is a COMPLETE STATEMENT OF WHAT THE EVIDENCE
   SUPPORTS. Adding a number would make it a weaker claim, not a stronger one,
   because the number would have no source.
```

**Caption.** Four boundary families, four reasoned directions, and the same tag on every arrow. The
refusal is drawn, not footnoted — which is the only way a diagram can be honest about a limit.

### The mechanism, properly built

```text
   WHY A BOUNDARY MOVES WHEN THE SCHEME DOES NOT

   ① ✅ A CLASS IS A THRESHOLD TEST ON A NORMAL  (Lessons 3 and 6)
             │
             │  📄 and the normal is "UPDATED EVERY DECADE to keep up with any
             │     changes in climate that might have taken place during the
             │     period" — IMD Preface, PDF p.5. THE AGENCY SAYS THE OBJECT MOVES.
             ▼
   ② IF THE 30-YEAR STATISTIC CHANGES, THE TEST RETURNS A DIFFERENT ANSWER
             │
             │  ✗ WITHOUT ANY THRESHOLD HAVING BEEN REVISED. Köppen's numbers
             │    have been fixed since 1936.
             ▼
   ③ THE MAP CHANGES  ──►  ④ AND EVERY DECISION BUILT ON THE OLD MAP IS NOW
                               BUILT ON AN INVALIDATED BASELINE
                               │
                               ├─ ⚠️ crop-suitability zoning
                               ├─ ⚠️ forest-type mapping
                               ├─ ⚠️ irrigation design
                               └─ ⚠️ pest-and-disease range assumptions
                               (✅ C1 lines 178-181, kept — this is C1's
                                strongest analytical passage)
             │
             ▼
   ⑤ ►► THE REAL ADMINISTRATIVE CONSEQUENCE IS NOT THAT THE CLIMATE CHANGED.
         IT IS THAT THE PLANNING BASELINE SILENTLY EXPIRED.
         That sentence is worth more in an answer than any shift statistic,
         AND IT REQUIRES NO DATASET TO MAKE.
```

### ✗ U3 to U7, applied — the five demotions and refusals, each shown

This is the only lesson in the edition where the **refusals are the syllabus**. Each is printed with
the owner's own words so that the student recognises the sentence if they meet it.

| # | ✗ C1's sentence | Why it fails | What this edition does instead |
|---|---|---|---|
| **U3** | ✅-marked: *"Koppen-Geiger maps: modern high-resolution versions (used by IPCC-era studies) track boundary migration decade-by-decade — the visual evidence of climate change."* | **No dataset, no version, no resolution, no release year.** No local book and no retrieved page supplies any of them (B2) | ⚠️ **Demoted.** The **mechanism** — that a threshold map re-drawn on new normals will differ — is kept; **the dataset implication is dropped** |
| **U4** | ✅-marked: *"Policy link: NAPCC + state action plans now realign crop planning, drought preparedness and water management to updated climate maps."* | **Unsourced, and not Geography's to teach** | ↪ **Routed out**, not demoted. Owner: `Environment-and-Ecology/basic/20_India-Climate-Policy-NAPCC-Panchamrit-LTLEDS`. **Named here, taught nowhere** |
| **U5** | ✅-marked: *"Thar's BWh belt is projected to expand eastward and southward; BSh steppe spreading into peninsular interior."* | **A projection with no dataset, baseline, scenario or period — and it contradicts C1's OWN lines 106 and 183–186** | ✗ **Refused outright.** No Indian geography is named as moving anywhere in this file. **This is the single most dangerous line in either owner** |
| **U6** | Table cell: *"New trend (2025/26)"* | **A dated-volatile label with no issuing agency** | ✗ **The temporal label is removed.** No sentence in this file carries an undated trend |
| **U7** | Table headed *"Old zone (1990s)"* / *"New trend (2025/26)"* | **The headings imply a measured shift; the two body cells do not contain one** | ✗ **Reframed as threshold sensitivity**, which is what C1's own cells actually say: *"sensitive to both precipitation seasonality and temperature"* and *"direction and magnitude depend on dataset and emissions scenario"* |

**Note the structure of C1's self-contradiction, because it is instructive.** ✅ C1 line 106 says:
*"Do not quote a national percentage shift without the underlying map, baseline and scenario."* ✅ C1
lines 183–186 say: *"do **not** quote a percentage of area shifting class, a projected zone boundary
for a given year, or a national-scale reclassification statistic."* ✗ C1 line 105 then quotes a
projected zone boundary. **The file knows the rule and breaks it one line earlier.** That is worth
pointing out in an answer about source handling: **a caution placed after a claim does not retract
it.**

### 🇮🇳 India hook 16 — the Thar margin, as mechanism and nothing more

**Where would a threshold move first, and why?** The reasoning is legitimate, and it can be carried
all the way to a place-name **as long as the claim stays at the level of sensitivity**:

```text
   WHY THE MARGIN IS THE SENSITIVE PLACE — A SENSITIVITY ARGUMENT, NOT A FORECAST

   ✅ B is defined by a RELATION (folio 158), so it has TWO independent causes
      of movement where every other boundary has one.
                    │
   ✅ The Thar margin sits at 12-25 cm (BShw) beside under 12 cm (BWhw)
      — folio 160. THE CLASS INTERVAL IS 13 cm WIDE.
                    │
   ✅ And at under 20 cm, rainfall variability is "around 60 per cent"
      — Majid 4.28. THE YEAR-TO-YEAR RANGE EXCEEDS THE CLASS INTERVAL.
                    │
                    ▼
   ⚠️ THEREFORE: this is the part of India where a classification boundary is
      MOST SENSITIVE to any change in either pan of the balance.
                    │
                    ▼
   ✗ AND THEREFORE NOT: that it HAS moved. Or WILL move. Or in WHICH direction
     on the ground. Or BY HOW MUCH. Or BY WHEN.
     ►► U5 IS REFUSED. NO DIRECTION OF EXPANSION IS ASSERTED FOR THE THAR
        ANYWHERE IN THIS FILE.
```

> **Stated limitation of this hook.** ⚠️ **Sensitivity is not movement.** The argument above says
> only that *if* either input changes, this margin is where the class assignment flips first — which
> follows from the width of the interval and the size of the variability, both sourced. **It says
> nothing about whether anything has changed.** No dataset, baseline, scenario or period is available
> locally, and **direction may be reasoned while magnitude may not be asserted.**

### What *can* be said, in full — the permitted claim set

Because this lesson is mostly refusals, the **permitted** claims are listed positively so that a
candidate knows what remains:

| ✅ / ⚠️ | Permitted claim |
|---|---|
| ✅ | **Köppen's thresholds have not changed since 1936** (Khullar folio 158, with the edition chronology from Lesson 2) |
| ✅ | **A class is a threshold test on a thirty-year normal** (Lessons 3 and 6) |
| 📄 | **IMD updates normals every decade *"to keep up with any changes in climate that might have taken place during the period"*** (Preface, PDF p.5) |
| ✅ | **Group B is defined by a relation, so a temperature rise alone can produce a dry class with no fall in rainfall** (folio 158) |
| ⚠️ | **Thermal boundaries move poleward and upslope by construction**, because the threshold is a temperature |
| ⚠️ | **Seasonality letters can change with no change in annual total** |
| ⚠️ | **A moved class invalidates the planning baseline built on the old one** — crop zoning, forest typing, irrigation design, pest-range assumptions (C1 lines 178–181) |
| 📄 | **The data themselves carry stated limits**: some elements rest on records *"which may be less than 30 years"*; phenomenon frequencies have *"an element of subjectivity"* (IMD, PDF p.7) |
| ✗ | **Everything else.** No percentage, no projection, no named moving boundary, no date |

### Comparison, objection, reply, residual

| | |
|---|---|
| **Comparison** | ✅ **Köppen's boundary** moves when a threshold is crossed. ✅ **Thornthwaite's** moves when an index band is crossed — and because the P/E index **sums twelve monthly ratios**, it responds to a change in **seasonality** even at constant annual totals. ✅ **Stamp's** moves only if the **January 18 °C isotherm** moves, which makes it the **simplest to monitor and the crudest**: one line, one month |
| **Objection** | *"Refusing every number makes the answer vague. Would an examiner not reward a concrete figure?"* |
| **Reply** | An examiner rewards a **defensible** figure. ✅ C1 itself forbids the unsourced kind at lines 106 and 183–186 — the rule is the owner's own, not an invention of this edition. And the alternative is not vagueness: *"a class is a threshold test on a thirty-year normal, the normal is revised decadally, and a revised class invalidates the planning baseline built on the old one"* is **concrete, mechanistic and completely sourced**. **Precision about what is known is not the same as reticence** |
| **Unresolved residual** | **The whole empirical question.** ✗ No dataset, baseline, scenario, period or study was retrieved. ✗ `https://cra.iitm.res.in/` returned a **DNS failure**. ✗ Four `mausam.imd.gov.in` and `internal.imd.gov.in` paths returned **HTTP 404**. **The edition therefore has a mechanism and no measurement, and says so** |

### Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ **Quoting a percentage of India's area reclassified.** ✗ Refusal 1, and C1's own rule.
- ❌ **Naming a direction of expansion for the Thar.** ✗ **U5** refused.
- ❌ **Attaching a year to a projected boundary.** ✗ Refusal 2, and C1 lines 183–186.
- ❌ **Citing "Köppen-Geiger high-resolution datasets".** ✗ **U3 / B2** — no version, resolution or
  release year exists in this evidence base.
- ❌ **Teaching NAPCC, LT-LEDS or the NDC.** ↪ **U4** — Environment-and-Ecology owns them.
- ❌ **Writing "New trend (2025/26)".** ✗ **U6** — no agency, no date.
- ❌ **Treating sensitivity as movement.** The Thar margin is the **most sensitive** boundary; that is
  not a claim that it has moved.
- ❌ **Assuming only rainfall decline can expand an arid class.** ✅ C1 line 113 is right and is kept:
  because B is **aridity-defined**, rising temperature can do it with stable rainfall.

### Syllabus, PYQ and answer-use

**Syllabus.** This lesson is where the *salient features* clause acquires a time dimension without
leaving Geography for Environment and Ecology — and the boundary between the two is exactly the
route-out table below.

**PYQ position.** ↪ **Topic 14 owns no routed PYQ.** The climate-change demands in the window are all
owned elsewhere: **2025 GS-I Q4**, *"Climate change and sea-level rise affecting island nations"*,
*Discuss*, 10 marks, routed to
`Environment-and-Ecology/basic/17_Climate-Change-Science-Greenhouse-Effect`
(`_PYQ-ROUTING-MAINS-GS1-GS2-ESSAY-2024-2025.md` line 63); **2021 GS-I Q15**, *"Melting Arctic ice
and Antarctic glaciers and weather patterns"*, *Explain*, 15 marks, routed to
`Geography/basic/25_Arctic-or-Polar-Climate` (line 184); and the **2018 Essay** prompt *"Alternative
technologies for a climate change resilient India"*, routed to
`Essay/advanced/03_Issue-Based-Prompt-Scoping` (line 412, printed in the ledger as *"Essay · 125
marks · 1000-1200 words"*, quoted as printed and not corrected here). **None is answered.** Topic 14
supplies one transferable instrument to all three: **the threshold-crossing argument**, which
explains how a classification can register change without the scheme being revised.

**Answer use.** The highest-scoring move is to convert the climate-change question into a
**baseline-invalidation** question, which is both more precise and fully sourced: *"The administrative
consequence of a moving class boundary is not the movement itself but the silent expiry of every
zoning decision built on the previous normal."*

### Revision points

1. **A class boundary is drawn on a statistic, not on the ground.** When the statistic moves, the
   boundary moves — **with no change to the scheme**.
2. ✅ **Köppen's thresholds have been fixed since 1936.** Any difference between two Köppen maps is a
   difference in the **data**.
3. 📄 **IMD, Preface PDF p.5:** normals are *"updated every decade to keep up with any changes in
   climate that might have taken place during the period"* — **the agency's own statement that the
   object moves**.
4. ✅ **Group B is the sensitive case** because it is defined by a **relation**: ✅ *"excess of
   evaporation over precipitation"* (folio 158). **Two independent causes of movement where every
   other boundary has one.**
5. ⚠️ **Four direction statements, all by construction and none quantified:** thermal limits poleward
   and upslope · arid classes assignable from either pan · polar and highland classes contracting
   from below · **seasonality letters changeable at constant annual totals**.
6. ⚠️ **The real consequence is baseline invalidation** — crop zoning, forest typing, irrigation
   design, pest-range assumptions (C1 lines 178–181).
7. ✗ **U5 refused: no Indian geography is named as moving anywhere in this file**, in any direction,
   at any rate, for any period.
8. ✗ **U3, U4, U6, U7 demoted, routed out or reframed.** ✅ **C1 forbids unsourced shift claims at its
   own lines 106 and 183–186 — and breaks the rule at line 105.** A caution placed after a claim does
   not retract it.
9. 🇮🇳 ⚠️ **The Thar margin is the most *sensitive* boundary in India** — a **13 cm** class interval
   (12–25 cm BShw against under 12 cm BWhw) against **around 60 per cent** variability. **Sensitivity
   is not movement.**

### Concept check

**Question:** Explain how a climatic classification can register climate change without any threshold
in the scheme being altered, and state precisely the limit of what may be claimed on that basis from
the available evidence.

**Model answer:** **The mechanism.** A Köppen class is not an observation; it is the **result of a
test** — ✅ *coolest month above 18 °C*, *warmest below 10 °C*, *coldest below −3 °C*, or ✅ for group
B, *"excess of evaporation over precipitation"* (Khullar folio 158). The test is applied to a
**thirty-year climatological normal**, not to a reading (Lesson 6). **If the normal changes, the test
returns a different answer and the boundary relocates — although Köppen's numbers have not moved
since 1936.** 📄 IMD's own Preface concedes the premise, stating that normals are *"updated every
decade to keep up with any changes in climate that might have taken place during the period"*. Group
**B** is the most responsive case, because it is defined by a **relation**: warming loads the
evaporative-demand side and can assign a dry class **without any fall in rainfall**. The
**seasonality letters** are responsive in a second way — a regime can keep its annual total and
change its slot-2 letter. **The limit.** What may be claimed is **direction by construction** and
**sensitivity by interval width** — the Thar margin's **13 cm** class interval against **around 60
per cent** variability makes it the most sensitive boundary in India. What may **not** be claimed is
any **magnitude, rate, extent, date or named moving boundary**: no dataset, baseline, scenario or
period exists in this evidence base, which is precisely why C1's ✅-marked claim that the Thar's BWh
belt *"is projected to expand eastward and southward"* is refused here — and why C1's own lines 106
and 183–186 forbid it.

**Misconception to avoid:** That a sound mechanism licenses a quantitative conclusion. The
threshold-crossing argument establishes that a boundary *can* move and *where it would move first*;
it establishes nothing about whether it *has*, which requires a named dataset with a stated baseline
and scenario — and is a different kind of claim entirely.

### Original Mains practice — Lesson 16

**Original question (10 marks, 150 words).** *Examine the limits of* using climatic classification as
evidence of climate change.

**Model (≤ 150 words):** The method is sound. A class is a **threshold test on a
thirty-year normal**, and Köppen's thresholds have been fixed since **1936**; so a map redrawn on new
normals differs only because the data differ. IMD itself updates normals **every decade** *"to keep
up with any changes in climate"*. Group **B** is most responsive, being defined by a **relation** —
evaporation against precipitation — so warming alone can assign a dry class with rainfall unchanged;
and the seasonality letters can change at constant annual totals. **The limits are severe.** The
underlying data are self-qualified: some elements rest on records *"which may be less than 30 years"*.
A boundary is an interpolation between stations, so its position reflects network density.
**No direction, rate, extent or date may be asserted without a named dataset, baseline and scenario**.
The defensible claim is **baseline invalidation**: zoning built on an expired normal silently fails.
*(146 words)*

**Rubric (10 marks):** Threshold mechanism **3.5** · Refusal discipline **3.0** ·
Baseline-invalidation argument **2.0** · Direction without magnitude **1.5**
*(dimension weights 35 · 30 · 20 · 15)*

Progress: 17 / 17 · Stage: Synthesis · Visual grammar: **argument tree doubling as an answer spine** · India hook: **windward Ghats against leeward Deccan**

### The intuition, before any term

Sixteen lessons have produced five maps of one country. **A candidate who can only recite them has
learned an inventory. A candidate who can say why they differ has learned the topic.**

They differ for exactly one reason, and it is worth stating before anything else: **each scheme can
only express what its notation has room for.**

- ✅ Köppen's three slots can record **thresholds and seasonality**, so Köppen sees the west coast as
  one region.
- ✅ Stamp's regional names can record **amount and annual range**, so Stamp splits that coast at Goa.
- ✅ Thornthwaite's double index can record **water availability month by month**, so Thornthwaite
  sees India as a moisture range at one temperature province.
- ✅ Trewartha's **H** declines to resolve the Himalaya at all, where Köppen gives three regions.
- ✅ R.L. Singh's ten divisions can record **two temperature ranges and a rainfall band per division**,
  the densest quantification of the five.

**The maps differ because the questions differ** — and that sentence is the thesis of the final
lesson and of any *"critically examine"* answer in this topic.

**Caption.** A thesis, five evidence branches, a limits branch, an alternatives row and a graded
conclusion. Read top to bottom it is an argument; written out in order it is a fifteen-mark answer.

### The five-scheme comparison, assembled

| | ✅ **Köppen** | ✅ **Trewartha** | ✅ **Thornthwaite 1931** | ✅ **Stamp (and Kendrew)** | ✅ **R.L. Singh** |

| **Basis** | Thresholds on monthly and annual means, placed where **native vegetation** changes | *"Modified and simplified"* Köppen | **P/E index** (Σ of 12 monthly ratios) **and T/E index** | The **January 18 °C isotherm**, then rainfall amount and regime | Temperature of the **hottest and coldest months** plus **average annual rainfall** |
| **World structure** | **5 groups** A–E | **6 groups** A–F **plus H** | 5 humidity × 4 seasonal × 6 T/E = **120**; **32** world types | — (India only) | — (India only) |

| **Resolves the west coast into** | **1** — Amw | **1** — Am | **1** — AA′r | **2** — Konkan and Malabar | **1** — Humid Sahyadri and West Coast |

| **+ *"Evaluate the data basis of Indian climatic classification"*** | **Normals**: WMO's thirty-year definition, decadal updating, **405** observatories, 1991–2020, with the stated caveats | Quote a station value, or reconcile 405 / 416 / 433 |

### 🇮🇳 India hook 17 — the windward Ghats against the leeward Deccan

✅ C1 lines 219–224 carry the topic's master evidence unit, and the audit found it **correctly
marked and analytically sound**. It is reproduced here with the source figures this edition has
added:

> **Claim.** Climate classification is an argument about causes disguised as a system of labels.
>
> **Evidence.** The windward Western Ghats and the leeward Deccan interior **share a latitude** and
> fall into **different Köppen groups** — ✅ **Amw at over 300 cm** against ✅ **BShw at 12–25 cm**
> (Khullar folio 160), a ratio above **twelve to one** across a single ridge. ✅ Stamp records the
> same contrast differently: the **Malabar Coast at over 250 cm** against the **Tropical *Moderate
> Rainfall* region at about 75 cm** in the Ghats' rain-shadow (Majid folio 4.33).
>
> **Significance.** It demonstrates that **relief and wind direction, not latitude, organise India's
> climatic map** — the premise of almost every Indian regional-climate question.
>
> **Limitation.** ✗ **The classification records the outcome without explaining the monsoon mechanism
> that produces it**, which is why a genetic account must be supplied alongside — and that account
> belongs to `Geography/advanced/16_India-Monsoon-Mechanism`, not here.

> **Stated limitation of this hook.** It is a **single contrast**, and a single contrast cannot carry
> a whole evaluation. Its value is that it is **simultaneously sourced in two schemes and two books**,
> which is rare in this topic; its limit is that it demonstrates **what classification cannot do**,
> not what it can.

### The topic's standing charge, and the honest reply

| | |
|---|---|
| **The charge** | **Köppen is causally silent.** It has no term for a mountain, no term for a wind direction, no term for a circulation reversal. It records that Amw and BShw sit either side of a ridge and offers no reason |
| **The reply, in three parts** | **① That silence is the price of reproducibility.** A scheme that explained causes would need causal data, which do not exist for every station on Earth. **② The silence is not ignorance but division of labour** — ↪ the monsoon mechanism is Topic 16's, the jet stream Topic 13's, forcing Environment-and-Ecology's. **③ And the scheme's own premise already concedes it**: ✅ Köppen took *native vegetation as the best expression of the totality of climate*, which is an admission that the **outcome** is being read rather than the **process** |
| **The residual** | ⚠️ **The genetic branch remains unquantified in this evidence base.** No local book prints a genetic classification table and no retrieved page supplies one. It is named in Lesson 1 and never given numbers — which is itself a limitation of this edition and is stated as one |

### Comparison, objection, reply, residual

| | |
|---|---|
| **Comparison** | Five schemes, **five different Indian answers**: 9 Köppen regions · 7 Trewartha sub-types · 12 Thornthwaite types · 11 Stamp regions · 10 Singh divisions. **None is a subset of another**, and each is finest exactly where its chosen variables vary most |
| **Objection** | *"If five schemes give five answers, is the whole enterprise not arbitrary?"* |
| **Reply** | **No — it is purposive.** Each scheme's output follows deterministically from its inputs and thresholds; the differences are traceable to **declared choices**, not to noise. The test of a classification is not whether another scheme agrees with it but whether **the same data, run through the same rules, give the same answer** — which all five satisfy. **Arbitrary would mean unrepeatable; these are repeatable and merely different** |
| **Unresolved residual** | **Four conflicts remain open at the close of this edition, by design:** ⇄ **Stamp 1953 against 1958/1961** (C13) · ⇄ **"Per Humid" against "Humid" North-East** (C19) · ⇄ **405 / 416 / 433** (Lesson 6) · ⇄ **Khullar's Et and E, not separable by the printed thresholds** (C2). **None is resolved, because nothing in the evidence base adjudicates them** |

### Traps specific to this lesson

- ❌ **Ranking the schemes.** They answer different questions; name the question.
- ❌ **Merging two schemes' Indian results.** ✗ **C14** — the topic's commonest and costliest error.
- ❌ **Writing an evaluation with no thesis.** ✅ C1 §9.2 supplies one: **operational, not complete**.
- ❌ **Listing nine regions and stopping.** A *critically examine* answer needs the **limits** branch
  and the **alternatives** row.
- ❌ **Claiming Köppen explains the monsoon.** ✗ It records the outcome.
- ❌ **Resolving a conflict the sources leave open.** Four stand open and should be **named as open**.
- ❌ **Importing the Essay framing.** ↪ The **2024 Essay** prompt *"Forests precede civilizations and
  deserts follow them"* is routed to `Essay/basic/03_Issue-Based-Prompt-Scoping`; Topic 14 supplies
  only the aridity-threshold material that makes the second clause arguable.
- ❌ **Treating the coverage package as a source.** It is a coverage and ordering map only and is
  cited nowhere except the `# COVERAGE MATRIX`.

### Syllabus, PYQ and answer-use

**Syllabus.** This lesson closes the *salient features of world's physical geography* clause's
classification half and hands the type-level content to Topics 15 and 17–25, which own it.

**PYQ position.** ↪ **Topic 14 owns no routed PYQ, and this is the lesson where that fact becomes an
argument rather than an absence.** The topic is the **grammar** of five owners that *are* tested:
**2021 Prelims Q60** and **2023 Prelims Q63** (`Geography/basic/15_Hot-Wet-Equatorial-Climate`),
**2021 Prelims Q61** (`Geography/basic/17_Savanna-Sudan-Climate`), **2024 Prelims Q13**
(`Geography/basic/22_Cool-Temperate-Western-Margin-British-Type`) and **2021 GS-I Q15**
(`Geography/basic/25_Arctic-or-Polar-Climate`). **All five are type-identification demands that a
classification-literate candidate reaches faster, and none is answered here.** The ledgers' neutrality
wording is preserved throughout and **no key, option letter or elimination chain appears anywhere in
this file.**

**Answer use.** Use the tree. Seven nodes, seven paragraphs: **thesis → scheme → application →
strength → limit → alternatives → graded conclusion.** For a 20-mark answer, expand the alternatives
row into two paragraphs; for 10 marks, compress to thesis, application and limit. **The structure
does not change; only the depth does.**

1. **The master thesis:** ✅ **Köppen survives because it is OPERATIONAL, not because it is
   complete** (C1 §9.2, surviving the audit intact).
2. **Why the five maps differ: each scheme can only express what its notation has room for.**
3. ✅ **Köppen-India: 9 regions, groups A B C D E, no H.** ✅ **Trewartha-India: 4 types, 7 sub-types,
   H present.** ✅ **Thornthwaite-India: 12 of 32 world types.** ✅ **Stamp-India: 11 regions in 2
   halves.** ✅ **Singh-India: 10 divisions.**
4. **Resolution is local:** Köppen gives the Himalaya **three** regions and the west coast **one**;
   Stamp gives the Himalaya **one** and the west coast **two**.
5. ✅ **Transferable: Köppen, Trewartha, Thornthwaite.** ✗ **Not transferable: Stamp, R.L. Singh** —
   their regions are places, not classes.

   direction or a circulation reversal.

   the same latitude** — a ratio above twelve to one across one ridge (folio 160). ✅ Stamp records
   the same contrast as **over 250 cm** against **about 75 cm** (Majid 4.33).
9. **Relief and wind direction, not latitude, organise India's climatic map.**

    and deletes **Dfc**, **Et** and **E**.

### Concept check

**Question:** Construct, in outline, the argument you would use for a 15-mark question asking for a
critical examination of Köppen's classification with reference to India — and state at each stage
what evidence you would cite.

**Model answer:** **Seven stages.** **① Thesis.** Köppen endures because it is **operational, not
complete**. **② The scheme.** Wladimir Köppen, University of Graz; **1901, 1918, 1931, 1936**;
empirical thresholds on monthly and annual means, placed where **native vegetation** changes; five
groups on three numbers — **18 °C, 10 °C, −3 °C** — with **B** defined by a relation, *"excess of
evaporation over precipitation"*. **③ The application.** **Nine** Indian regions across **A, B, C, D
and E**: Amw over 300 cm, As 75–100 cm, Aw about 75 cm, BShw 12–25 cm, BWhw under 12 cm, Cwg with
summers to 40 °C, **Dfc** in Sikkim and Arunachal, Et in Uttarakhand, E in higher Jammu & Kashmir —
**and no H**. **④ The strength.** Computable from any station register, globally comparable, and
sharp enough to separate the windward Ghats from the leeward Deccan **at the same latitude**. **⑤ The
limit.** It records without explaining — no term for relief, wind direction or monsoon reversal; it
draws hard lines across gradients; and it is data-bound, since a class is a test on a **thirty-year
normal** that IMD revises **every decade**. **⑥ The alternatives.** Trewartha's six-plus-H, whose
Indian answer is **A, B, C, H in seven sub-types** and whose **H is "undifferentiated"**;
Thornthwaite's **P/E and T/E** indices with **5 × 4 × 6 = 120** combinations; Stamp's **eleven**
regions on the **January 18 °C isotherm**; Singh's **ten** divisions. **⑦ Graded conclusion.** Retain
Köppen as the descriptive frame, supplement it with a water-balance approach for agricultural
application, read the Indian regionalisations for what codes cannot carry, and treat every boundary
as a moving line.

**Misconception to avoid:** That a critical examination requires a verdict against the scheme.
"Critically" asks for the **basis of judgement**, not for condemnation; an answer that establishes
what question Köppen answers, what it necessarily cannot answer, and what the alternatives buy and
cost has examined it critically without ever declaring it wrong.

### Original Mains practice — Lesson 17

**Original question (20 marks, 250 words).** *Critically examine* the proposition that no single
climatic classification can adequately represent India.

**Model (≤ 250 words):** **The proposition is true, and the reason is structural.**
A classification can express only what its notation has room for, so the five schemes
applied to India give five incompatible answers — Köppen **nine regions**, Trewartha **seven
sub-types**, Thornthwaite **twelve of thirty-two** world types, Stamp **eleven regions**, R.L. Singh
**ten divisions** — and none is a subset of another.

**The differences are diagnostic.** Köppen's three slots record thresholds and seasonality, so the
west coast south of Mumbai is one region, **Amw, over 300 cm**. Stamp can record **annual range**, so
the same coast splits at Goa into the **Konkan** (over 200 cm, range 3–6 °C widening northward) and
the **Malabar** (over 250 cm, range 3 °C). In the mountains the advantage reverses: Köppen resolves
**Dfc, Et and E**, while Trewartha assigns a single *"undifferentiated"* **H**. Thornthwaite alone
expresses water availability, placing **AA′r** and **EA′d** at opposite ends of one moisture axis
within a single temperature province.

**Adequacy is relative to purpose.** Köppen is computable from any station register and globally
comparable; Singh's divisions supply planning vocabulary and two temperature ranges per division;
Stamp supplies a structuring cut. Each is adequate to its own question.

**Qualified conclusion.** The proposition holds for **any single** scheme and fails as an argument
against classification itself. India requires a **portfolio**: Köppen as the comparable descriptive
frame, a water-balance scheme for agriculture, and the Indian regionalisations for variables codes
cannot carry — with every boundary read as a line drawn on a revisable statistic. *(246 words)*

**Rubric (20 marks):** Thesis **4.0** · Five-scheme comparison **5.0** · India application **4.0** ·
Limits and alternatives **4.0** · Graded conclusion **3.0**
*(dimension weights 20 · 25 · 20 · 20 · 15)*

| The monsoon mechanism that the classification records but does not explain | `Geography/advanced/16_India-Monsoon-Mechanism` |
| Each world climatic type taught in its own right | `Geography/basic/15`, `17`–`25` |

# CUMULATIVE CONCEPT CHECKS

**Mode note.** This edition runs in **concept-check mode** under the governing rules, which state:
*"Use concept-check mode; do not create a compiled four-option MCQ corpus."* **There is therefore no
four-option corpus anywhere in this file, no option letter and no key.** The checks below are
cumulative and cross-lesson; each is answerable only from material already taught, and none repeats a
lesson-local check.

**CC-1 · Scheme boundaries.** *Prompt:* A single Indian tract appears as **BShw** in one scheme, as
**Bs** in another, and inside the **Semi-Arid Tropical** division in a third. Name all three schemes,
and state what each code or name is actually asserting.
**Response:** ✅ **BShw** is **Köppen** (Khullar folio 160) — dry group by the evaporation relation,
steppe rather than desert, hot, with a dry winter, at **12–25 cm** in the Ghats' rain-shadow and much
of Rajasthan. ✅ **Bs** is **Trewartha** (folio 163) — semi-arid steppe, above 27 °C, rainfall below
100 cm, in the Ghats' rain-shadow. ✅ **Semi-Arid Tropical** is **R.L. Singh's division 8** (folio
165) — Gujarat, Maharashtra, Madhya Pradesh, Chhattisgarh, Karnataka and Telangana, January
**13–29 °C**, July **26–42 °C**, **50–100 cm**. The first two assert **class membership under
transferable thresholds**; the third asserts **a planning region of one country**. ✗ They are not
synonyms and must not be written as one code.

**CC-2 · Counting.** *Prompt:* Give the Indian region or type count for all five schemes taught here,
and say which single count is most often reported wrongly and why.
**Response:** ✅ Köppen **9** regions · ✅ Trewartha **7** sub-types from **4** types · ✅ Thornthwaite
**12** of 32 world types · ✅ Stamp **11** regions in two halves · ✅ R.L. Singh **10** divisions. ✗
**The Köppen count is the one reported wrongly**, as **seven**, because C2's table collapses **Dfc**
out of existence and merges **Et**, **E** and an imported **H** into one row (C15). **A/B/C/H is
Trewartha's result and importing it deletes three Köppen regions** (C14).

**CC-3 · Letters across schemes.** *Prompt:* The letter **E** appears in Köppen, in Trewartha and in
Thornthwaite. State what it means in each.
**Response:** ✅ **Köppen: E = polar**, warmest month below **10 °C**, subdivided **T** tundra and
**F** icecap (folios 158–159). ✅ **Trewartha: E = boreal**, with **F** as polar (folio 162) — the
letter has shifted one position. ✅ **Thornthwaite 1931: E = the arid humidity province**, P/E index
**under 16**, and **E′** is the **tundra temperature province** at 1–15 (folio 161). **Three schemes,
three meanings, one letter** — which is why every code must be read with its scheme named.

**CC-4 · Source auditing.** *Prompt:* Name the three places in Khullar's Chapter 5 where the printed
apparatus contradicts the printed application, and say which can be settled internally.
**Response:** ✗ **One:** folio 159's *"h (heiss) = average annual temperature under 18 °C"* against
folio 160's **BWhw** in *"most of western Rajasthan"* — **settled internally**, since the assignment
refutes the rule (C1). ✗ **Two:** folio 160's **Et** at 0–10 °C against **E** at warmest month
0–10 °C — **not settled**; the two are the same interval and by the folio-159 **T/F** rule a polar
region reaching 0–10 °C is **ET** (C2). ✗ **Three:** folio 163's **Bsh** called a *"desert"* at
**50–100 cm** — **settled internally**, because **S is steppe** and the figure is steppe-consistent
(C3). ✗ A fourth, **Bwh** as a *"middle latitude desert"*, is settled by **cross-reference** to the
same book's Köppen section, which codes the tract **BWhw** (C4).

**CC-5 · Data discipline.** *Prompt:* Three official IMD surfaces give three station counts. State
them with their surfaces, say which controls, and state what must not be done.
**Response:** 📄 **405** — the publication, *Climatological Tables of Observatories in India
1991-2020*, **Ninth Edition (2022)**, Preface PDF p.5, *"including WMO centennial stations"*.
📄 **416** — `www.imdpune.gov.in/library/publicationnew.html`, in a description attached to **1981–2010**
printed beneath a heading reading *"Climatological Normals: (1991 - 2020)"*. 📄 **433** —
`dsp.imdpune.gov.in/home_normals.php`, five-digit selector options, counted 2026-10-04. **The
publication controls**, because it carries an edition, a date and a signatory. ✗ **No arithmetic may
be performed between the three** — no subtraction, ratio, trend or explanation — because nothing in
the retrieved evidence says they count the same objects.

**CC-6 · Version discipline.** *Prompt:* A statement reads: "Thornthwaite's scheme uses a moisture
index of precipitation against potential evapotranspiration." Say what is missing and why it matters.
**Response:** **The version.** ✅ Khullar folio 160 dates the scheme **1931**, revised **1933** and
**1948**, and says *"the 1948 scheme was **markedly different**"*. The statement describes **1948**;
the source **tabulates 1931** — a **P/E ratio**, a **P/E index** of twelve summed monthly ratios,
five humidity provinces, four seasonal subtypes, six **T/E** provinces, **120** combinations. It
matters because the familiar trap *"Thornthwaite is moisture-based, not temperature-based"* is
**false of 1931**, which uses **both** indices (C10).

**CC-7 · Map discipline.** *Prompt:* Name the four maps that must be kept apart, the input of each,
and the kind of boundary each draws.
**Response:** ✅ **Climate class** — input a **thirty-year normal**, boundary a **threshold**.
↪ **Vegetation biome** — input observed cover, boundary an **ecotone**, and it records disturbance.
↪ **Agro-climatic zone** — input climate **plus soil, irrigation and cropping history**, boundary
frequently **administrative**. 📄 **Hazard and vulnerability** — input **event frequency in decadal
windows plus exposure and coping capacity**, boundary a **risk contour over a population**. ✅ C1
lines 237–240 names them as *"separate units"*. ✗ The Atlas's live title is **"Hazard Atlas of
India"**, not C2's *"Climate Hazards and Vulnerability Atlas of India"* (U8).

**CC-8 · Open conflicts.** *Prompt:* Four conflicts stand open at the close of this edition. Name
them and say why each is open.
**Response:** ⇄ **Stamp's date** — **1953** as a joint Stamp-and-Kendrew scheme (Majid 4.28) against
**1958** and **1961** as separate works (Khullar bibliography); a scheme date and two publication
dates are **different objects** and nothing adjudicates (C13). ⇄ **Division 1's name** — *"Per Humid
North-East"* (Khullar 164) against *"Humid North-East"* (Majid 4.36); **no third source** (C19).
⇄ **405 / 416 / 433**; no source explains the divergence. ⇄ **Khullar's Et and E**, which carry the
**same printed interval** and cannot be separated by the printed criterion (C2). **Contrast C18**,
which *is* closed: Khullar's *"HUMID SOUTH WEST"* legend is corrected by Majid's *"Humid South-East"*
map **and** by the geography of Bengal, Odisha, Chhattisgarh and Jharkhand.

**CC-9 · Refusal discipline.** *Prompt:* State four claims this topic invites and this edition
refuses, with the reason for each.
**Response:** ✗ **A percentage of India's area reclassified** — no dataset, baseline or scenario.
✗ **A direction of expansion for the Thar's BWh belt** — C1 line 105 asserts one with a ✅ and
contradicts its own lines 106 and 183–186 (U5). ✗ **A Köppen-Geiger map version, resolution or
release year** — no local book and no retrieved page supplies one (U3, B2). ✗ **An "IMD
classification of India into climatic zones"** — the normals publication tabulates **stations**, the
Hazard Atlas maps **hazards**, and neither classifies (B6).

**CC-10 · Synthesis.** *Prompt:* In one construction, explain why five schemes produce five
incompatible Indian maps without any of them being wrong.
**Response:** **Because each scheme can express only what its notation has room for.** ✅ Köppen's
three slots carry thresholds and seasonality, so the west coast south of Mumbai is **one** region,
**Amw, over 300 cm**. ✅ Stamp's regional names carry **amount and annual range**, so the same coast
is **two** regions, **Konkan** (over 200 cm, range 3–6 °C widening northward) and **Malabar** (over
250 cm, range 3 °C). ✅ In the mountains it reverses: Köppen resolves **Dfc, Et, E** where ✅
Trewartha assigns one *"undifferentiated"* **H**. ✅ Thornthwaite alone expresses water availability,
placing **AA′r** and **EA′d** at opposite ends of one moisture axis in a single temperature province.
**The maps differ because the questions differ, and each scheme is finest where its chosen variables
vary most.**

# ORIGINAL 10-, 15- AND 20-MARK MAINS MODEL PRACTICE

**All seven are ORIGINAL.** None paraphrases a routed demand closely enough to function as an answer
to one. **Directives are pairwise distinct and distinct from all seventeen lesson-local directives.**
**Word caps are hard: 10-mark models ≤ 150 words; 15- and 20-mark models ≤ 250 words.** Every rubric
dimension-set below is distinct from the seventeen lesson-local sets and from the other six here.

## F1 — ORIGINAL · 10 marks · 150 words · **Elucidate**

**Question.** *Elucidate* the proposition that group B occupies a different logical position in
Köppen's scheme from groups A, C, D and E.

**Model.** A, C, D and E are **intervals on a single thermometer**: A takes a coolest month above
**18 °C**; C a coldest month below 18 °C but above **−3 °C** with a warmest above **10 °C**; D a
coldest below −3 °C; E a warmest below 10 °C. Each is a **level**. **B is a relation** — *"excess of
evaporation over precipitation"* — and therefore occupies no position on that axis. Three
consequences follow. **It is tested first and overrides**: a hot place that is dry is B, not A.
**It cuts across every height**, so a Thar desert and a mid-latitude cold desert share a group letter.
And **it has two independent causes of movement**, since either pan of the balance can tip it, which
no thermal boundary has. India shows it: **BWhw** at under **12 cm** sits in western Rajasthan, among
the hottest tracts in the country. *(149 words)*

**Rubric (10 marks):** Mechanism statement **3.5** · Terminology precision **3.0** · Worked instance
**2.0** · Brevity **1.5** *(dimension weights 35 · 30 · 20 · 15)*

## F2 — ORIGINAL · 10 marks · 150 words · **Substantiate**

**Question.** *Substantiate*, from named sources, the claim that India's climatic map is organised by
relief and wind direction rather than by latitude.

**Model.** Four pieces of evidence, all from printed folios. **One.** Khullar's Köppen-India places
**Amw at over 300 cm** on the west coast south of Mumbai and **BShw at 12–25 cm** in the Ghats'
rain-shadow — **the same latitudes, a ratio above twelve to one, across one ridge**. **Two.** The
same list gives the Coromandel **As at 75–100 cm**, its rain arriving in **winter from the retreating
monsoon**, so two tropical coasts at similar latitudes have opposite water years. **Three.** Majid's
Stamp-and-Kendrew figures corroborate under a different scheme: **Malabar over 250 cm**, **Konkan
over 200 cm with over 90 per cent from the Arabian stream**, against the Ghats' leeward *Moderate
Rainfall* region at **about 75 cm**. **Four.** Khullar's **Dfc** in Sikkim and Arunachal is a
cold-winter Köppen region inside tropical latitudes, produced by **height**. *(148 words)*

**Rubric (10 marks):** Evidence density **4.0** · Folio attribution **2.5** · Counter-instance
**2.0** · Closure **1.5** *(dimension weights 40 · 25 · 20 · 15)*

## F3 — ORIGINAL · 10 marks · 150 words · **Comment**

**Question.** *Comment* on the status of highland climates in the schemes applied to India.

**Model.** The position is unstable across schemes and is routinely misreported. **Köppen's group
list has five groups — A, B, C, D, E — and no H**; H is a later addition, as both standard treatments
correctly note. Köppen nevertheless **resolves India's mountains finely**, assigning **Dfc** to
Sikkim, Arunachal and parts of Assam with winters about **10 °C**, **Et** to the Uttarakhand mountain
areas at **0–10 °C**, and **E** to higher Jammu & Kashmir and Himachal Pradesh. **Trewartha** does
the opposite: he adds **H, "undifferentiated climate of highlands"**, and assigns the entire belt —
Jammu & Kashmir, Himachal, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal — to it. The common claim that *India has
Köppen A, B, C and H* therefore transplants **Trewartha's** result and deletes three Köppen regions.
A qualification is owed: Khullar's **Et** and **E** share one printed interval and are not separable
as printed. *(150 words)*

**Rubric (10 marks):** Position taken **3.0** · Qualification **3.0** · Supporting datum **2.5** ·
Restraint **1.5** *(dimension weights 30 · 30 · 25 · 15)*

## F4 — ORIGINAL · 15 marks · 250 words · **Discuss**

**Question.** *Discuss* the role of climatological normals in the construction and the instability of
climatic regions.

**Model.** **Construction.** A climatic class is not observed; it is **computed**. Every Köppen
threshold — 18 °C, 10 °C, −3 °C — is applied to a **normal**, defined by WMO and reproduced by IMD as
*"arithmetic averages of the climatological data computed for consecutive period of **thirty
years**"*. India's current series is the **ninth edition (2022)**, covering **1991–2020** for **405
meteorological observatories including WMO centennial stations**, with readings at **0830 and 1730
IST**. The provenance chain runs observatory → reading → thirty-year mean → threshold test → class →
map → use, and a classification enters only at the fifth stage.

**Instability.** The same document concedes the problem: normals are *"**updated every decade** to
keep up with any changes in climate that might have taken place during the period"*. If the normal
moves, the class moves — **without any threshold being revised**. Group **B** is most responsive,
being a **relation** rather than a level. Density matters too: the series ran on **171** stations in
**1904** and **235** for **1931–60**, and a boundary is an interpolation between stations.

**Qualifications.** The data are self-limiting — some elements rest on records *"which may be less
than 30 years"*, and phenomenon frequencies carry *"an element of subjectivity"*. Official surfaces
even diverge on the count: **405** in the publication, **416** and **433** on two IMD portals, all
accessed 2026-10-04. **Reported side by side; no difference computed.** *(248 words)*

**Rubric (15 marks):** Scope coverage **4.5** · Two-sided treatment **3.75** · Indian instantiation
**3.75** · Source-conflict handling **1.5** · Structure **1.5**
*(dimension weights 30 · 25 · 25 · 10 · 10)*

## F5 — ORIGINAL · 15 marks · 250 words · **Analyse**

**Question.** *Analyse* the relationship between rainfall amount and rainfall reliability in India,
and its bearing on climatic classification.

**Model.** **The relationship is inverse and is stated by the source.** Majid records that desert
tracts — **Barmer, Ganganagar, Jaisalmer, Jodhpur** — receive **less than 20 cm** with variability
**around 60 per cent**; the **100–200 cm** belt of the Ghats' western slopes, Lesser Himalayas,
Shiwaliks and Tarai shows **10–20 per cent**; and areas above **200 cm**, including **Mawsynram and
Cherrapunji**, show **less than 10 per cent**. The source's own generalisation: *"there is an
**inverse relationship** between the average annual rainfall and variability in rainfall."*

**Decomposition.** Amount and reliability are therefore **not independent descriptors**; the driest
places are also the least predictable, so aridity is **unreliability compounded by shortage**.

**Bearing on classification.** Köppen's Indian dry pair is separated by a narrow margin — **BShw at
12–25 cm** against **BWhw at under 12 cm** — a **13 cm** interval. At **60 per cent** variability the
**year-to-year range exceeds the class interval**, so the same station can satisfy either criterion
depending on which years the normal spans. The boundary is a **belt**, and the scheme draws it sharp
only because a classification must assign every point.

**Residual.** Thornthwaite's design anticipates this by summing **twelve monthly P/E ratios** rather
than using one annual figure, but requires **evaporation** data. Köppen prints **no aridity formula
at all**, so the Indian classes can be read from the map and not recomputed from the rule. *(249
words)*

**Rubric (15 marks):** Decomposition **4.5** · Causal linkage **3.75** · Data integration **3.75** ·
Residual naming **3.0** *(dimension weights 30 · 25 · 25 · 20)*

## F6 — ORIGINAL · 20 marks · 250 words · **Evaluate**

**Question.** *Evaluate* Köppen's classification against the criteria a classification of climate
should satisfy.

**Model.** **Criteria declared first:** reproducibility, comparability, resolution where variation is
greatest, explanatory adequacy, and stability.

**Reproducibility — strong.** Inputs are monthly and annual means of temperature and precipitation,
available for every station; thresholds are fixed numbers — 18 °C, 10 °C, −3 °C, 22 °C — so two
analysts reach the same class.

**Comparability — strong.** A code means the same thing everywhere, which Stamp's *"Konkan Coast"*
and Singh's *"Sub-Humid Littorals"* cannot.

**Resolution — mixed.** Köppen resolves India's mountains into **three** regions where Trewartha
gives one undifferentiated **H**; but he gives the west coast **one** region where Stamp distinguishes
**Konkan** from **Malabar** on amount and annual range, which his notation cannot carry.

**Explanatory adequacy — weak, by design.** The scheme has no term for relief, wind direction or
circulation. It records that **Amw at over 300 cm** and **BShw at 12–25 cm** lie either side of one
ridge at the same latitude and offers no reason.

**Stability — conditional.** Thresholds have not moved since **1936**, but classes rest on
**thirty-year normals** that IMD revises **decadally**, so a class is only as stable as its reference
period.

**Verdict.** Köppen satisfies the operational criteria and fails the explanatory one — which is what
it was designed to do. **It should be retained as the comparable descriptive frame and supplemented**,
not replaced: a water-balance scheme for agricultural questions, the Indian regionalisations for
variables codes cannot hold. *(248 words)*

**Rubric (20 marks):** Criteria declared **5.0** · Weighed against criteria **5.0** · Evidence spread
**4.0** · Refusal discipline **3.0** · Verdict **3.0**
*(dimension weights 25 · 25 · 20 · 15 · 15)*

## F7 — ORIGINAL · 20 marks · 250 words · **Critically discuss**

**Question.** *Critically discuss* the use of printed classificatory sources, with reference to the
schemes applied to India.

**Model.** **The problem.** Classificatory material reaches a candidate through secondary texts whose
apparatus and application can disagree — and in this topic they repeatedly do.

**Evidence, across schemes.** Khullar's Köppen letters define *"h (heiss) = average annual
temperature **under** 18 °C"*, while his own Indian list assigns **BWhw** to *"most of western
Rajasthan"*, the country's hottest desert — **the application refutes the apparatus**. His **Et**
(0–10 °C) and **E** (warmest month 0–10 °C) carry the **same interval** and are not separable as
printed. His Trewartha sub-types call **Bsh** a *"desert"* although **S is steppe** and the printed
**50–100 cm** is steppe-consistent, and **Bwh** a *"middle latitude desert"* although the same book
codes the tract **BWhw**. His R.L. Singh map legend prints *"HUMID SOUTH WEST"* where his text reads
**Humid South-East**. His contents page swaps Trewartha and R.L. Singh, and **FIG. 5.27 is printed
twice**.

**Objection and reply.** One might conclude the source is unusable. That overcorrects: every defect
above was **detected from within the same chapter or from a second local book**, which is precisely
what a reliable source permits.

**Limits of the evidence base.** Some conflicts cannot be closed — Stamp dated **1953** against
**1958** and **1961**; *"Per Humid"* against *"Humid"* North-East.

**Qualified close.** Cite by **printed folio**, quote the apparatus **and** the application, close
what cross-checking closes, and **report the rest open**. *(249 words)*

**Rubric (20 marks):** Problem framing **4.0** · Multi-scheme evidence **4.0** · Objection and reply
**5.0** · Limits of the evidence base **4.0** · Qualified close **3.0**
*(dimension weights 20 · 20 · 25 · 20 · 15)*

# REMEDIATION

**How to use this section.** Each row names an error, the lesson that owns the fix, and the shortest
corrective action. The errors are ordered by **cost in an examination**, not by lesson number.

## Tier 1 — errors that change the answer

| # | Error | Owned by | Corrective action |
|---:|---|---|---|
| R-01 | **Writing "India has Köppen A, B, C and H."** | **L9, L10** | **A/B/C/H is Trewartha's** (folio 163). Köppen-India is **A B C D E, nine regions, no H** (folios 158, 160). Re-read the altitudinal column in L9 and the crosswalk in L10. **This single error deletes Dfc, Et and E** |
| R-02 | **Giving Köppen-India seven regions.** | **L9** | Nine. The missing two are **Dfc** (Sikkim, Arunachal, parts of Assam, winter about 10 °C) and the **Et / E** pair. Write the nine-item list from memory until it is automatic |
| R-03 | **Reproducing "h (heiss) = under 18 °C."** | **L4, L8** | ✗ Inverted. **The proof is internal**: Khullar's own **BWhw** sits in western Rajasthan. State ⚠️ **h ≥ 18 °C, k < 18 °C**, and show the contradiction |
| R-04 | **Describing Thornthwaite without naming a version.** | **L11, L12** | **1931 is tabulated; 1948 is described.** The trap *"moisture-based not temperature-based"* is **false of 1931**, which uses a P/E **and** a T/E index |
| R-05 | **Quoting an unsourced climate-shift claim.** | **L16** | Refused. Direction may be reasoned; **magnitude, rate, extent and date may not be asserted**. C1's own lines 106 and 183–186 forbid it |

## Tier 2 — errors that lose marks without changing the answer

| # | Error | Owned by | Corrective action |
|---:|---|---|---|
| R-06 | **Reading `w` as "wet" or `s` as "snow".** | **L5** | Slot 2 is the **dry-season** slot. `w` = dry winter, `s` = dry summer, `m` = monsoon short dry season, `f` = none |
| R-07 | **Reading `g` as a rainfall letter.** | **L2, L5, L9** | ✅ **The Ganges type of annual march of temperature** — hottest month **before** the solstice and the rains |
| R-08 | **Writing Cwa as the source code.** | **L9** | ✅ Khullar prints **Cwg**. ⚠️ Cwa is the modern-map equivalent. **Name both and name the convention** |
| R-09 | **Citing "Fig. 5.27."** | **L9, L13** | ✗ Printed **twice** — Stamp map (folio 157) and Köppen map (folio 159) — while the body cites **Fig. 5.28**. **Cite by printed folio** |
| R-10 | **Using Khullar's contents page.** | front matter | ✗ Trewartha and R.L. Singh are **swapped**. Body folios only |
| R-11 | **Citing "Moderate Rainfall" without its half.** | **L13** | ✗ Stamp's legend prints it for **region 4 (Temperate/Continental)** and **region 8 (Tropical)** |
| R-12 | **Writing "Humid South West" for Singh's division 3.** | **L14** | ✗ Khullar's **legend** is the error; his body, Majid's Fig. 4.24 and the geography all give **Humid South-East** |
| R-13 | **Calling Trewartha's groups "tropical, dry, subtropical…"** | **L10** | ✗ That is **Trewartha-Horn (1980)**. Khullar's 1954 list is **A B C D E F plus H**, with **E boreal** and **F polar** |
| R-14 | **Treating 12 cm and 25 cm as Köppen thresholds.** | **L8** | They are **Khullar's Indian regional figures**, not criteria |
| R-15 | **Subtracting one IMD station count from another.** | **L6** | ✗ Refused. Report all three with URLs and the access date |
