# COMPLETE CONSOLIDATED REGISTER NOTES

**These are compressed revision notes covering all seventeen lessons, organised by the topic's own
structure rather than by a generic template. They are the final teaching section of this edition.**

## Why a classification exists, and the four rival bases

- A classification is a **decision about what to measure**, taken before measuring. Two climate maps
  of one country differ because that decision differed.
- **Four bases, four questions.** ✅ **Vegetation-anchored** (Köppen) · ✅ **latitude-and-position
  anchored** (Leong) · ✅ **water-balance anchored** (Thornthwaite) · ⚠️ **genetic / air-mass**
  (named, unquantified in this evidence base).
- ✅ **Leong, book p.114:** six zones — **0–10° · 10–30° · 30–45° · 45–65° · 65–90° · Alpine** — and
  **twelve** numbered types, imperial rainfall, **no thresholds, no letter codes**. Chapter ends at
  p.115 with *"QUESTIONS AND EXERCISES"*.
- ✗ **Leong carries no Köppen at all.** Index K-block: *Kalmuk · Karst Lakes · Karst (region) ·
  Kazakhs · Khamsin · Kirghiz · Knick point · Koum*, then L. **C1's grounding line is false for
  Köppen** (U1). ✗ Majid *IWG*: one classification-adjacent hit in 488 pages, `isohyet`, a **Topic 13**
  object.
- **Köppen's content in this topic is Khullar's, printed folios 156–167.**

## The Indian lineage, and the first attempt

- ✅ **Majid *GoI* folio 4.28 — ten authorities:** 1 **H.E. Blandford 1889** · 2 **W. Köppen 1918,
  1931, 1936** · 3 **C.W. Thornthwaite 1931, 1933, 1948** · 4 **L.D. Stamp and W.G. Kendrew 1953** ·
  5 **S.P. Chatterji 1953** · 6 **G.T. Trewartha 1954** · 7 **V.P. Subramanyam 1956** · 8 **B.L.C.
  Johnson 1969** · 9 **K.L. Rao et al. 1971** · 10 **R.L. Singh 1971**.
- ✅ **Blandford — the first Director General of IMD** — made the first systematic Indian climatic
  division in **1889** and *"discovered that all types of climates found in the world are present
  within the subcontinent of India"*.
- ✅ **The source's own criticism:** it rested on *"temperature and rainfall of a few selected
  stations"* and was *"almost an overgeneralisation."*
- **Five of the ten are taught here in full: Köppen, Thornthwaite, Stamp-and-Kendrew, Trewartha,
  R.L. Singh.** The other five are named; **no local folio carries their content**.

## Köppen: identity, editions, premise

- ✅ **Wladimir Köppen, University of Graz, Austria** (folio 158).
- ✅ **1901 first published · 1918 and 1931 major revisions · 1936 latest work.** ✗ Majid's list
  begins at 1918; **1901 is Khullar's** (C12).
- ✅ **Bibliography (folio 166):** *Grundriss der Klimakund* **[sic]** (1931), Walter de Gruyler
  Company, Berlin; *Das Geographische System der Klimate*, *Handbuch der Klimablogie* **[sic]**
  (1936), *"Ed. W. Koppen and **R. Geiger**, Vol. I, Berlin"*.
- **"Köppen-Geiger" rests on that 1936 co-editorship and on nothing else.** ⚠️ **No map version,
  resolution or release year exists in this evidence base** (U3, B2).
- ✅ **The premise:** empirical, from **annual and monthly means of temperature and precipitation**,
  with **native vegetation accepted as the best expression of the totality of climate**. Three stacked
  claims: inputs are **numbers**; the warrant is **plants**; the justification is that plants
  **integrate everything**. **The word *native* is the scheme's own admitted limit.**

## The five groups, and the one relation

- ✅ **A** — tropical rainy, no cool season, **coolest month above 18 °C**.
- ✅ **B** — **dry**, *"excess of evaporation over precipitation"*. **A relation, not an interval.
  Tested first; overrides.**
- ✅ **C** — mild winters, **coldest month below 18 °C but above −3 °C**, warmest **over 10 °C**.
- ✅ **D** — severe winters, **coldest below −3 °C**, warmest **above 10 °C**.
- ✅ **E** — polar, **warmest month below 10 °C**; capitals **T** tundra, **F** icecap.
- ✅ **H is NOT in Khullar's group list.** Five groups. **A later addition** — C1 L25 and C2 L14 both
  say so correctly.
- ⇄ **C5:** the C/D boundary is **−3 °C** in Khullar; ⚠️ many modern maps use **0 °C**. **State the
  convention** (C1 line 74's own formulation, kept).
- **22 °C is not a group boundary** — it splits the third letter.
- **Three numbers do the work: 18, 10, −3.** One month decides the group; eleven are discarded.

## The dry group, and an inverted letter

- ✅ **S = steppe · W = desert** (folio 159) — positions along one relation.
- ✗ **C1: folio 159 prints "h (heiss) — average annual temperature *under* 18 °C."** ✗ **The internal
  proof:** ✅ folio 160 puts **BWhw** in *"most of western Rajasthan"* and **BShw** on the Thar
  margin. ⚠️ **Corrected: h ≥ 18 °C mean annual, k < 18 °C.**
- ✗ **C6: `k` appears nowhere in Khullar's list.** ⚠️ **BWk** and **BSk** are standard knowledge with
  no local folio. ✅ C1 line 79 is kept: B *"can be hot (BWh/BSh) or cold (BWk/BSk)"*.
- ✗ **Khullar prints no aridity formula.** The Indian classes can be read from the map and **not
  recomputed from the rule**. Thornthwaite 1931 is the answer to that complaint.

## The code grammar, slot by slot

- **Slot 1** group · **Slot 2** when the dryness falls · **Slot 3** how hot the summer or the dryness
  is.
- ✅ **Printed list, folio 159: "a, c, f, h, m, g, s and w."**
- ✅ **a** warmest month **over 22 °C** · ✅ **c** warmest month **under 22 °C** · ✅ **f** no dry
  season · ✅ **w** dry **winter** · ✅ **s** dry **summer** · ✅ **m** monsoon, short dry season.
- ✅ **g — the Ganges type of annual march of temperature**: the hottest month comes **before the
  solstice and the summer rainy season**. **A temperature letter, not a rain letter.**
- ✅ Capitals: **S/W** subdivide **B**; **T/F** subdivide **E**.
- ✗ **C6: b, d and k are named in neither the list nor the definitions.** a and c alone **do not
  partition** C and D. **Every Indian code is readable; Dfb, Dfd, Cfb, Csb, BWk and BSk are not.**
- **India's dry codes take a fourth character** — **BShw**, **BWhw** — the trailing `w` being the
  dry-winter letter. The grammar used fully, not an exception.

## Normals: the data under every map

- 📄 **IMD, *Climatological Tables of Observatories in India 1991-2020*, Ninth Edition (2022)**, 844
  pp, issued **July 2022**; Preface **Mrutyunjay Mohapatra**, DGM; Acknowledgements **Krishnanand
  Hosalikar**, Head CR&S, Pune; compiled by the **National Data Centre**, Pune-411005.
- 📄 **WMO definition:** normals are *"arithmetic averages of the climatological data computed for
  consecutive period of **thirty years**"*. 📄 **Updated every decade** *"to keep up with any changes
  in climate that might have taken place during the period."*
- 📄 **The series:** first normals **1904**, **171** stations · fourth publication **1931-60**,
  **235** stations · present **1991–2020**, **405** observatories *"including WMO centennial
  stations"*.
- 📄 **Observation hours: 0300 UTC (0830 IST) and 1200 UTC (1730 IST).**
- 📄 **Two stated caveats:** phenomenon frequencies rest on *"visual observations and have an element
  of subjectivity"*; some normals come from records *"which may be less than 30 years"*. 📄 **Extremes
  use a different span** — all data from the observatory's start to the end of **2020**.
- ⇄ **Three official surfaces, three counts, all 2026-10-04: 405** (publication) · **416**
  (`imdpune.gov.in/library/publicationnew.html`, described against **1981-2010** under a **1991 -
  2020** heading) · **433** (`dsp.imdpune.gov.in/home_normals.php`, selector options). **The
  publication controls. No arithmetic is performed.**
- ✗ **"Climatological Tables 1–405" on the contents page is a PAGE RANGE**, not a station count.
- ✗ **No retrieved IMD surface classifies India into climatic zones.**

## Köppen's nine Indian regions

- ✅ **1 Amw** — monsoon with a short dry winter; **west coast south of Mumbai**; **over 300 cm**.
- ✅ **2 As** — dry season in the high-sun period; **Coromandel / coastal Tamil Nadu and adjoining
  Andhra**; **75–100 cm**, mostly **winter rain from the retreating monsoon**.
- ✅ **3 Aw** — tropical savanna; peninsular plateau **barring the Coromandel and Malabar strips**;
  northern limit **roughly the Tropic of Cancer**; **about 75 cm**.
- ✅ **4 BShw** — semi-arid steppe; **Ghats' rain-shadow**, much of **Rajasthan with contiguous
  Haryana and Gujarat**; **12–25 cm**.
- ✅ **5 BWhw** — hot desert; **most of western Rajasthan**; **less than 12 cm**.
- ✅ **6 Cwg** — monsoon with dry winter; most of the **Ganga Plain**, eastern **Rajasthan**,
  **Assam**, **Malwa Plateau**; **summer to 40 °C, winter 27 °C**.
- ✅ **7 Dfc** — cold humid winters, shorter summer; **Sikkim, Arunachal Pradesh, parts of Assam**;
  **winter about 10 °C**. **Absent from both owners.**
- ✅ **8 Et** — tundra; **mountain areas of Uttarakhand**; **0–10 °C**.
- ✅ **9 E** — polar; **higher Jammu & Kashmir and Himachal Pradesh**; **warmest month 0–10 °C**.
- **NINE REGIONS · FIVE GROUPS A B C D E · NO H.**
- ✗ **C14:** A/B/C/H is **Trewartha's**. ✗ **C15:** C2's seven-row table loses **Dfc** and merges the
  rest. ✗ **U9:** India's D and E regions are **montane but genuine**, not absences.
- ✗ **C2:** **Et** and **E** carry the **same 0–10 °C interval**; by the **T/F** rule a polar region
  reaching 0–10 °C is **ET**. **Safe form: *"E-group conditions (ET and, above the snow line,
  EF)"*.**
- ⇄ **C16:** ✅ Khullar prints **Cwg**; ✗ C1 says **Cwa**. **Teach Cwg, explain g, present Cwa as the
  ⚠️ modern-map equivalent.**
- ✗ **Figure citation:** the body cites **Fig. 5.28** while the map at folio 159 is **FIG. 5.27** —
  the same number as the Stamp map at folio 157. **Never cite a Khullar figure number alone.**

## Trewartha: six groups, four Indian types, seven sub-types

- ✅ **Prof. Glenn T. Trewartha, University of Wisconsin**, *An Introduction to Climate*; *"modified
  and simplified"* Köppen (folio 162).
- ✅ **A** tropical humid · **B** dry · **C** humid mesothermal · **D** humid microthermal · **E
  boreal** · **F polar** · **H undifferentiated climate of highlands**. ✗ **E means polar in Köppen
  and boreal in Trewartha.**
- ✗ **C11:** C2's *"subtropical"* group is **Trewartha and Horn (1980)**, cited separately in Khullar's
  bibliography alongside **Trewartha (1954)** and **Trewartha (1961), *The Earth's Problem Climates***.
  ⚠️ The 1980 content is a bibliography line; **no table from it is reproduced**.
- ✅ **India: *"four types viz. A, B, C and H … divided into seven sub-types"*** (folio 163).
- ✅ **Am** 27 °C, **over 250 cm**, western coastal plain **and Tripura** · ✅ **Aw** 27 °C, **about
  100 cm** all in summer, peninsular plateau **and Mizoram** · ✅ **Bs** semi-arid steppe, above
  27 °C, **below 100 cm**, Ghats' rain-shadow · ✗ **Bsh** printed *"tropical and subtropical
  **desert**"*, **50–100 cm** — **C3: S is steppe and the figure is steppe-consistent** · ✗ **Bwh**
  printed *"**middle latitude** desert"*, west Rajasthan and Kachchh, to **20 cm** — **C4: h is hot,
  and the same book codes that tract BWhw** · ✅ **Caw** most of the northern plain, winter **below
  18 °C**, summer rain **decreasing east to west**, **western disturbances** giving light winter rain
  · ✅ **H** undifferentiated highlands: **J&K, Himachal, Uttarakhand, Sikkim, Arunachal**.
- **Köppen resolves the Himalaya into three regions; Trewartha into one.** ✅ Map **FIG. 5.30**.

## Thornthwaite 1931: the tabulated scheme

- ✅ **Presented 1931, revised 1933 and 1948**; the first two **similar**; *"the 1948 scheme was
  **markedly different**"* (folio 160).
- ✅ **P/E ratio** = monthly precipitation ÷ evaporation. ✅ **P/E index** = **the sum of the twelve
  monthly P/E ratios** — not an annual ratio.
- ✅ **Humidity provinces:** **A** wet/rainforest **128+** · **B** humid/forest **64–127** · **C**
  subhumid/grassland **32–63** · **D** semiarid/steppe **16–31** · **E** arid/desert **under 16**.
- ✅ **Seasonal subtypes:** **r** adequate all seasons · **s** deficient in summer · **w** deficient in
  winter · **d** deficient in all seasons.
- ✅ **T/E provinces:** **A′** tropical **128+** · **B′** mesothermal **64–127** · **C′** microthermal
  **32–63** · **D′** taiga **16–31** · **E′** tundra **1–15** · **F′** frost **0**.
- ✅ **120 combinations — 5 × 4 × 6 = 120, derivation shown.** ✅ **32 world types** and ✅ **12 Indian
  types** are **source counts: attribute, never justify** (C7).
- ✅ **India, certified readings:** **1 AA′r** west coast, Tripura, Mizoram · **2 BA′w** eastern
  slopes of the Western Ghats, West Bengal · **3 BB′w** Assam, Meghalaya, Mizoram, Nagaland ·
  **4 CA′w** large parts of peninsular India · ✗ **5** printed **CA′w′**, described as *"rainfall
  occurs in winter"* for Tamil Nadu and adjoining Andhra · ✗ **10** bare **D′** · ✗ **11** bare
  **E′** · ✅ **12 EA′d** western Rajasthan, *"extremely hot and dry"*, T/E **over 128**, P/E **below
  16**.
- ⚠️ **Items 6–9 are not held in this evidence base and are not reproduced.**
- ✗ **C9:** item 5's code contradicts its own description — by the book's **w / s** rule a winter-rain
  region is **CA′s**. **Describe the regime in words.**
- ✗ **C8:** folio 162 supplies type 12 and **completes** the enumeration. The "only eleven" hypothesis
  is **withdrawn**. ✅ Map **FIG. 5.29**.
- **Thornthwaite exists only in Khullar in this evidence base.**

## Thornthwaite 1948: the water-balance turn

- ✅ **Characterised in prose only** (folio 160): the **plant as a meteorological instrument**;
  **vegetation as the physical mechanism transporting water**; **evapotranspiration**.
- ⚠️ **No bands, index values or table for 1948 appear anywhere in this evidence base**, so the
  moisture-index formulation is **⚠️, not ✅**.
- ✗ **C10:** C2 lines 36, 45, 55 and C1 line 160 describe **1948** and cite a book that tabulates
  **1931**. ✗ C2 line 59's trap — *"moisture-index based, not temperature-based"* — is **false of
  1931**, which uses **both** indices. **Name the version before the basis.**
- ✅ **Bibliography (folio 167):** Thornthwaite **1933**, *"The Climates of the Earth"*, *Geographical
  Review* **23(3), 433–440**; **1948**, *"An Appraisal Towards Rational Classification of Climate"*,
  *Geographical Review* **38, 55–94**. ✗ **No 1931 entry at all** — a citation gap, **reported, not
  repaired**. ✅ Majid 4.28 independently dates him *"1931, 1933, 1948"*.
- ⚠️ **The rabi premise:** the northern plain's winter is dry **and cool**, so **demand falls with
  supply**, with ✅ **western disturbances** giving light winter rain. **Climate premise is Topic 14's;
  the crop is Economy's.**

## Stamp (and Kendrew): the split map

- ✅ **The divider: the JANUARY 18 °C ISOTHERM** (folio 156), separating **Temperate or Continental
  India** from **Tropical India**. 🇮🇳 **Same number as Köppen's A/C boundary, different job — no
  derivation claimed.**
- ✅ **Temperate/Continental India (5):** 1 **The Himalayan Region** · 2 **The North Western Region** ·
  3 **The Arid Lowland** · 4 **Moderate Rainfall** · 5 **The Transitional Zone** — ✅ eastern UP,
  western and north-western Bihar, north-western Jharkhand; **100–150 cm**; **over 90 %** from the
  **Bay of Bengal** stream; January ≈ **15 °C**; mean maximum July **over 40 °C** (Majid 4.33).
- ✅ **Tropical India (6):** 6 **Very Heavy Rainfall** — Assam, Meghalaya, Nagaland, Manipur, Tripura,
  Mizoram; **over 200 cm**; *"the heaviest rainfall in the world … at the stations of **Mawsynram and
  Cherrapunji**"*; over 90 % in the SW monsoon · 7 **Heavy Rainfall** — West Bengal, Orissa,
  Jharkhand, eastern Andhra; **100–200 cm decreasing east to west**; January **over 18 °C**; ≈ 30 °C
  in June–July · 8 **Moderate Rainfall** — east of the Ghats, Gujarat, SW MP, Maharashtra, Karnataka,
  greater Andhra; **rain-shadow**; **about 75 cm**; January ≈ 18 °C, July ≈ 32 °C · 9 **The Konkan
  Coast** — Tapi mouth to **Goa**; **more than 200 cm**, over **90 %** from the **Arabian** stream;
  January ≈ 24 °C, July ≈ 27 °C; **range 3–6 °C increasing south to north** · 10 **Malabar Coast** —
  **Goa to Cape Comorin**; **over 250 cm**; annual ≈ 27 °C, **range 3 °C**; **Kochi** typical ·
  11 **Tamil Nadu** — Coromandel; **100–150 cm**; most in the **retreating monsoon, October–December**;
  January ≈ 24 °C, July ≈ 30 °C.
- ✗ **C17: regions 4 and 8 share the identical legend label *"Moderate Rainfall"*.** Cite by **half
  and number**. ⚠️ **Region 4's figures are supplied by no local folio and are not invented.**
- ⇄ **C13:** ✅ Majid 4.28 — *"L.D. Stamp and W.G. Kendrew, **1953**"*; ✅ Khullar's map — *"After
  **Stamp**"*; ✅ Khullar's bibliography — **Stamp 1958** and **Kendrew 1961** separately; ✅ Khullar
  folio 164 — *"**Kendrew and Stamp**"*. **Present as "Stamp (and Kendrew)" with both datings.
  Unresolved.**
- **A regionalisation of one country, not a transferable classification.** ✅ Map **FIG. 5.27**.

## R.L. Singh 1971: ten divisions

- ✅ *"**Following Kendrew and Stamp**, Dr. R.L. Singh presented his climatic divisions of India in
  **1971**"* — ten divisions on **the temperature of the hottest and coldest months and the average
  annual rainfall** (folio 164). ✅ Majid 4.36: *"**modified** the climatic divisions of Stamp and
  Kendrew"*; *"**quantitative as well as qualitative**"*; *"the modifications have been done **on the
  basis of variations in temperature**."*
- ⇄ **C19: 1** — *"**Per Humid** North-East"* (Khullar) against *"**Humid** North-East"* (Majid body
  and Fig. 4.24). **Both given; neither chosen.** ✅ **Majid alone supplies its figures:** the whole of
  NE India **except Tripura**, Sikkim and north-western West Bengal; **more than 200 cm**; July
  **25–33 °C**; January **10–25 °C**.
- ✅ **2 Humid Sahyadri and West Coast** — January **19–28 °C**, July **26–32 °C**, **about 200 cm**.
- ✗ **C18: 3 Humid South-East** — Bengal, Odisha, Chhattisgarh, Jharkhand; **12–27 / 26–34 °C**;
  **100–200 cm**. ✗ **Khullar's FIG. 5.31 legend prints "HUMID SOUTH WEST" and is the error**;
  ✅ Majid's **Fig. 4.24** and the geography both confirm **South-East**. **The only defect in this
  topic closed outright by a second local source.**
- ✅ **4 Subhumid Transition** — eastern UP, Bihar, north Jharkhand; **9–24 / 24–41 °C**; **100–200 cm**.
- ✅ **5 Subhumid Littorals** — eastern Tamil Nadu, coastal Andhra; January **20–29 °C**, May
  **28–38 °C**; **75–150 cm**, mostly **November–December**, retreating monsoon.
- ✅ **6 Subhumid Continental** — Ganga plain; **7–23 / 26–41 °C**; **75–150 cm**. ⚠️ Majid's legend
  prints *"Humid continental climate"*; **both shown**.
- ✅ **7 Semi-arid Subtropical** — the **Satluj–Yamuna divide**: Punjab, Haryana, eastern Rajasthan,
  Delhi, Chandigarh; **25–100 cm**; January **6–23 °C**, May **26–41 °C**.
- ✅ **8 Semi-arid Tropical** — Gujarat, Maharashtra, MP, Chhattisgarh, Karnataka, Telangana; January
  **13–29 °C**, July **26–42 °C**; **50–100 cm**.
- ✅ **9 Arid** — Thar, west Rajasthan, SW Haryana, Kachchh; **about 25 cm, locally 10 cm**; January
  **5–22 °C**, June **20–40 °C**; **very large diurnal and annual range**.
- ✅ **10 West Himalayan** — J&K, Himachal, Uttarakhand; July **5–30 °C**, January **0–4 °C**;
  **150 cm**; **SW monsoon plus western disturbances**.
- ✅ **Bibliography:** *"Singh, R.L. (**ed.**) (1971): India: A Regional Geography, National Geog.
  Society of Indian"* **[sic]**. ✅ Map **FIG. 5.31** / Majid **Fig. 4.24**.
- **The vocabulary — arid, semi-arid, sub-humid, humid, crossed with continental, littoral,
  sub-tropical, tropical — is a planning vocabulary, not a climatological one. Do not code it.**

## Four maps, four questions

- ✅ **C1 lines 237–240, kept:** *"station normal, grid cell, climatic region, vegetation biome,
  agro-climatic zone and administrative boundary are **separate units**."*
- **① Climate class** — input a **thirty-year normal**, boundary a **threshold**. **② Vegetation
  biome** — input observed cover, boundary an **ecotone**, records **disturbance**. **③ Agro-climatic
  zone** — input climate **plus soil, irrigation, cropping history**, boundary frequently
  **administrative**. **④ Hazard/vulnerability** — input **decadal event frequency plus exposure and
  coping capacity**, boundary a **risk contour over a population**.
- ✗ **U8: the live title is "HAZARD ATLAS OF INDIA"**, maintained by the **Climate Application and
  user Interface Group (CAUI)**, IMD **Pune-05** — not C2's *"Climate Hazards and Vulnerability Atlas
  of India"*. 📄 Re-confirmed 2026-10-04, HTTP 200, 10,225 B.
- 📄 **Nine climate hazards:** Wind · Extreme rainfall · Lightning · Dust Storm · HailStorm · Fog ·
  Drought · Cyclone · Thunderstorm — plus a separate **Climate Vulnerability** section. 📄 **Decadal
  windows** (extreme rainfall 1951-1960 … 2011-2020, plus 1901-2019 and 1951-2019; cyclone 1961-1970
  … 2011-2020, plus 1961-2020). **A normal is a thirty-year mean; a hazard layer is a decadal count.**
- ✅ **C2's two surviving sentences, kept:** *"District risk can differ within one climate class"* and
  ⚠️ *"hazard becomes disaster through exposure and capacity."*
- ✗ **B6: no retrieved IMD surface classifies India into climatic zones.**

## Threshold mobility, and the refusals

- **A class boundary is drawn on a statistic.** When the statistic moves, the boundary moves — ✅
  Köppen's thresholds have been fixed since **1936**.
- 📄 IMD: normals are *"updated every decade to keep up with any changes in climate that might have
  taken place during the period"* — **the agency's own concession that the object moves.**
- ⚠️ **Four direction statements, none quantified:** thermal limits **poleward and upslope** · arid
  classes assignable **from either pan** · polar and highland classes **contracting from below** ·
  **seasonality letters changeable at constant annual totals**.
- ⚠️ **The administrative consequence is baseline invalidation** — crop zoning, forest typing,
  irrigation design, pest-range assumptions (C1 lines 178–181).
- 🇮🇳 ⚠️ **The Thar margin is the most *sensitive* boundary**: a **13 cm** class interval against
  **around 60 per cent** variability. **Sensitivity is not movement.**
- ✗ **Refused: U5** (Thar expansion, ✅-marked in C1 and contradicting C1's own lines 106 and
  183–186) · **U3** (Köppen-Geiger datasets) · **U6** and **U7** (*"New trend (2025/26)"* and the
  implied-shift table) · **U4** routed out to the NAPCC owner.

## Rainfall variability — the quantitative India hook

- ✅ **Majid *GoI* folio 4.28, Fig. 4.20:** **Barmer, Ganganagar, Jaisalmer, Jodhpur** below **20 cm**
  with variability **around 60 per cent**; **100–200 cm** belt (western slopes of the Western Ghats,
  Lesser Himalayas, Shiwaliks, Tarai) at **10–20 per cent**; **over 200 cm** (**Mawsynram**,
  **Cherrapunji**, Meghalaya Plateau) at **less than 10 per cent**.
- ✅ *"There is an **inverse relationship** between the average annual rainfall and variability in
  rainfall."*
- **Consequence:** near the **12 cm** Köppen line, the year-to-year range **exceeds** the class
  interval. **The boundary is a belt; the scheme draws it sharp only because it must.**

## The synthesis

- **Thesis:** ✅ **Köppen survives because it is operational, not because it is complete** (C1 §9.2,
  surviving the audit intact).
- **Why five maps differ: each scheme can express only what its notation has room for.**
- **The counts: Köppen 9 · Trewartha 7 sub-types from 4 types · Thornthwaite 12 of 32 · Stamp 11 ·
  Singh 10.** **None is a subset of another.**
- **Resolution is local:** Köppen gives the Himalaya **three** and the west coast **one**; Stamp gives
  the Himalaya **one** and the west coast **two**.
- 🇮🇳 ✅ **The master hook:** windward Ghats **Amw over 300 cm** against leeward Deccan **BShw
  12–25 cm**, **same latitude, one ridge, a ratio above twelve to one**. ✅ Stamp records the same
  contrast as **over 250 cm** against **about 75 cm**. **Relief and wind direction, not latitude.**
  ✗ **The classification records the outcome and does not explain the monsoon that produces it.**
- **The seven-node spine:** thesis → scheme → application → strength → limit → alternatives → graded
  conclusion.
- **The graded conclusion:** retain Köppen as the **descriptive frame**; supplement with a
  **water-balance** approach for agriculture; read **Stamp and Singh** for variables codes cannot
  carry; treat every boundary as a **moving line on a revisable statistic**.
- ⇄ **Four conflicts stand open: Stamp 1953 / 1958-61 · "Per Humid" / "Humid" North-East · 405 / 416
  / 433 · Khullar's Et and E.** **Name them as open.**
- ↪ **Topic 14 owns no routed PYQ and is the grammar of five owners that do** — 2021 Prelims Q60,
  2021 Q61, 2023 Q63, 2024 Q13, 2021 GS-I Q15. **The value is leverage, not frequency.**

**Text-layer reality.** Leong 11,045 extractable characters, **0 text-rich pages**. Khullar 82,052,
**0**. Majid *Geography of India* 8,320, **0** — every page's only text is the repeated
`http://www.visionias.net/` watermark. Majid *IWG* 651,975 across 347 pages, **OCR degraded**
(`3,36`, `pj4om ayy`) and usable for term sweeps only. **Three of four books were read by rendering
pages to images; no text was machine-extracted from them and none is claimed.**

**Khullar's scan geometry, recorded because every citation depends on it.** A **two-up rotated scan**;
`pdf_page = (folio + 34) // 2`, with the folio in the first half when `(folio + 34)` is even;
**rotation alternates page by page** — scan pages 95, 96, 98 and 99 render upright at 270°, 97 and
100 at 90°. Because changing the page rotation changes the coordinate system, clipping must be done
**after** rendering, in the image. **Every folio cited was confirmed by reading its printed number in
the rendered image.**

## Certified negative findings — established by inspection, not by absent search hits

1. **Leong contains no Köppen classification.** Book pp. 113–115 carry a **latitudinal twelve-type
   scheme**; the chapter ends at p.115 with *"QUESTIONS AND EXERCISES"*; **the index K-block has no
   Köppen entry**.
2. **Majid *Indian & World Geography* contains no classification section.** Chapter Three
   (Climatology) runs scan PDF 74–127 without one; Chapter Ten's climate folios are image-only; a
   whole-book term sweep returns **one** classification-adjacent hit, `isohyet` at scan PDF 124 — a
   **Topic 13** object.
3. **Majid *Geography of India* folios 4.29–4.30 (Köppen) and 4.34–4.35 (Trewartha) are absent from
   the local extract.** The contents page proves the sections exist in the complete book; **the local
   file does not contain them and nothing is quoted from them.**
4. **The map bank holds no classification asset.** Zero matches for Köppen, Trewartha, Thornthwaite,
   Stamp, Kendrew, R.L. Singh, climatic region, isohyet, isotherm, IMD, normals, Mawsynram or
   Cherrapunji; the physiographic anchors are present. **The gap is declared and the bank is not
   edited.**
